[mu_2024_1_P005_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P005 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
DRAM: DRAM products are dynamic random access memory semiconductor devices with low latency that provide high-speed data retrieval with a variety of performance characteristics.

[mu_2024_1_P005_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P005 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
DRAM products lose content when power is turned off ("volatile") and are most commonly used in the data center, client PC, graphics, industrial, and automotive markets.

[mu_2024_1_P006_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P006 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
In 2023, we began shipping the industry's first 1β (1-beta) production node, which offers further improvements in power efficiency and bit density.

[mu_2024_1_P006_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P006 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
The majority of our DRAM bit production in 2024 was on leading-edge 1α (1-alpha) and 1ß nodes.We expect volume shipments in 2025 of our next generation of DRAM, the 1γ (1-gamma) node, which will employ EUV lithography production.

[mu_2024_1_P007_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P007 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Double Data Rate ("DDR"): DDR memory transfers data twice per clock cycle resulting in improved speeds, power efficiency, and storage density.

[mu_2024_1_P007_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P007 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
DDR5 is the fifth generation of this technology and offers the critical improvements in bandwidth and power efficiency necessary to meet the growing needs of high-performance computing, AI, and data-intensive applications.

[mu_2024_1_P008_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P008 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
High-Bandwidth Memory ("HBM"): A 3D stacked DRAM architecture that utilizes through-silicon via ("TSV") connections for a more efficient communication between each stack giving it the ability to achieve a higher bandwidth while consuming less power compared to other memory types.

[mu_2024_1_P008_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P008 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
This makes it ideal for applications that require high data throughput and energy efficiency, such as AI applications and high-performance computing.

[mu_2024_1_P009_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P009 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Graphics DRAM ("GDDR"): High performance memory solution designed for graphics cards, gaming consoles, and high-performance computing applications.

[mu_2024_1_P009_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P009 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
GDDR memory is optimized for high-bandwidth workloads encountered by graphics processing units, offering faster data rates and efficient data processing capabilities.

[mu_2024_1_P010_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P010 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Low-Power DRAM ("LPDDR"): Engineered for mobile devices and applications requiring low power consumption.

[mu_2024_1_P010_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P010 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
LPDDR products generally operate at a lower voltage than standard DRAM products and are beneficial to any power conscious application.

[mu_2024_1_P010_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P010 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
The benefits of LPDDR memory are being realized by many market segments including mobile, PC, automotive, and data center.

[mu_2024_1_P012_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P012 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies

[mu_2024_1_P012_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P012 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies

[mu_2024_1_P016_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P016 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Solid State Drives ("SSDs"): SSD storage products incorporate NAND, a controller, and firmware to offer significant performance and features over hard disk drives, including smaller form factors, faster read and write speeds, higher reliability, and lower power consumption needed to address the growing demands of data-centric workloads, ever-increasing expectations of client users, and the stringent requirements of automotive and industrial applications.

[mu_2024_1_P017_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P017 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Managed NAND: Managed NAND combines NAND flash with a sophisticated controller and firmware in a single package.

[mu_2024_1_P017_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P017 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
This integration allows the memory to manage itself, handling tasks like wear leveling, bad block management, and error correction internally, freeing the host system from these tasks.

[mu_2024_1_P017_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P017 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Products such as embedded MultiMediaCards ("eMMC") and universal flash storage ("UFS") offer solutions that are compact and reliable making them widely used across the mobile, automotive, and industrial markets.

[mu_2024_1_P018_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P018 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
Multi-Chip Packages ("MCPs"): Designed to provide high-performance, compact, and efficient memory solutions by integrating multiple types of memory, generally LPDRAM, into a single package.

[mu_2024_1_P018_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P018 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies

[mu_2024_1_P020_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P020 - Item 1 - Products, Market, and Sales > Product Technologies
NOR is most commonly used for reliable code storage (e.g., boot, application, operating system, and execute-in-place code in an embedded system) and for frequently changing small data storage and is ideal for automotive, industrial, and consumer applications.

[mu_2024_1_P052_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P052 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
We manufacture our products within our own facilities located in Taiwan, Singapore, Japan, the United States, Malaysia, China, and India and also utilize subcontractors to perform certain manufacturing processes.

[mu_2024_1_P052_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P052 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Our products are manufactured on 300mm wafers in facilities that generally operate 24 hours per day, seven days per week.

[mu_2024_1_P052_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P052 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Semiconductor manufacturing is capital intensive, requiring large investments in sophisticated facilities and equipment.

[mu_2024_1_P052_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P052 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Our DRAM, NAND, and NOR products share a number of common manufacturing processes, enabling us to leverage our product and process technology and certain resources and manufacturing infrastructure across these product lines.

[mu_2024_1_P053_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Our process for manufacturing semiconductor products is complex and involves numerous precise steps, including wafer fabrication, post fabrication processing, assembly, and test.

[mu_2024_1_P053_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Efficient production of semiconductor products requires utilization of advanced semiconductor manufacturing techniques and effectively deploying those techniques across multiple facilities.

[mu_2024_1_P053_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
The primary determinants of manufacturing cost are process line-width, 3D non-volatile layers, NAND cell levels, process complexity (including the number of mask layers and fabrication steps), and manufacturing yield.

[mu_2024_1_P053_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Other factors include the cost and sophistication of manufacturing equipment, equipment utilization, cost of raw materials, labor productivity and cost, package type, cleanliness of our manufacturing environment, and utilization of subcontractors to perform certain manufacturing processes.

[mu_2024_1_P053_S005] Parent mu_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
As we continue to increase our production of high value products and solutions, manufacturing costs are increasingly affected by the costs of application-specific integrated circuit controllers and other semiconductors, advanced and complex packaging configurations, and testing at progressively higher performance speeds and quality levels.

[mu_2024_1_P053_S006] Parent mu_2024_1_P053 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
We continuously enhance our production processes, increase bits per wafer, transition to higher density products, and utilize advanced testing and assembly processes.

[mu_2024_1_P054_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Wafer fabrication occurs in a highly-controlled clean environment to minimize yield loss from contaminants.

[mu_2024_1_P054_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Despite stringent manufacturing controls, individual circuits may be nonfunctional or wafers may be scrapped due to equipment errors, minute impurities in materials, defects in photomasks, circuit design marginalities or defects, or contamination from airborne particles, among other factors.

[mu_2024_1_P054_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Success of our manufacturing operations depends largely on minimizing defects and improving process margin to maximize yield of high-quality circuits.

[mu_2024_1_P054_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
In this regard, we employ rigorous quality controls throughout the manufacturing, screening, and testing processes.

[mu_2024_1_P054_S005] Parent mu_2024_1_P054 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
We continue to heighten quality control as our product offerings expand into higher-end segments that require increasing performance targets.

[mu_2024_1_P055_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Manufacturing
Our products are manufactured and sold in both packaged form and as unpackaged bare die.

[mu_2024_1_P055_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P055_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P055_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P055_S005] Parent mu_2024_1_P055 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P056_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P056 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P056_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P056 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P057_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P057 - Item 1 - Manufacturing

[mu_2024_1_P067_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P067 - Item 1 - Resources > Patents and Licenses

[mu_2024_1_P067_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P067 - Item 1 - Resources > Patents and Licenses
We are unable to predict whether these license agreements can be obtained or renewed on terms acceptable to us.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Our R&D efforts are focused primarily on development of memory and storage solutions, including our industry-leading DRAM and NAND technology, that enable continuous improvement in performance and cost structure for our products.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Our 1-gamma DRAM pilot production using EUV lithography is progressing well, and we are on track for volume production in calendar 2025.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
We have begun volume production on our Micron G9 NAND node, and expect to introduce several industry-leading products over the coming quarters.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
We are well-positioned with our industry leading HBM3E technology.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S005] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
We are also focused on developing new and fundamentally different memory structures, materials, and packages designed to facilitate our transition to next generation products.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S006] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Additional R&D efforts are concentrated on the enablement of advanced computing, storage, and mobile memory architectures and the investigation of new opportunities that leverage our core semiconductor expertise.

[mu_2024_1_P068_S007] Parent mu_2024_1_P068 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Product design and development efforts include HBM, DDR5, LPDDR5, high-capacity MRDIMMs, CXL based products, and advanced graphics DRAM; TLC and QLC NAND technologies; mobile and storage solutions (including firmware and controllers) in managed NAND and SSDs; and other memory technologies and systems.

[mu_2024_1_P069_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P069 - Item 1 - Research and Development
To compete in the semiconductor memory and storage markets, we must continue to develop technologically advanced products and processes.

[mu_2024_1_P069_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P069 - Item 1 - Research and Development
The continued evolution of our semiconductor product offerings is necessary to meet expected customer requirements for memory and storage products and solutions.

[mu_2024_1_P069_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P069 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Our process, design, firmware, controller, package, and system development efforts occur at multiple locations across the world.

[mu_2024_1_P069_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P069 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Our primary R&D centers are located in Boise, Idaho; San Jose, California; India; Japan; Taiwan; China; Italy; Singapore; Germany; Malaysia; and other sites in the United States.

[mu_2024_1_P070_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P070 - Item 1 - Research and Development
R&D expenses vary primarily with the number of development and pre-qualification wafers processed and end-product solutions developed, personnel costs, and the cost of advanced equipment dedicated to new product and process development, such as investments in EUV lithography equipment.

[mu_2024_1_P070_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P070 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Because of the lead times necessary to manufacture our products, we typically begin to process wafers before completion of performance and reliability testing.

[mu_2024_1_P070_S003] Parent mu_2024_1_P070 - Item 1 - Research and Development
Development of a product is deemed complete when it is qualified through internal reviews and tests for performance, functionality, and reliability.

[mu_2024_1_P070_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P070 - Item 1 - Research and Development
R&D expenses can vary significantly depending on the timing of product qualification and the scope of the product.

[mu_2024_1_P083_S002] Parent mu_2024_1_P083 - Item 1 - Government Regulations > Environmental Compliance

[mu_2024_1_P083_S007] Parent mu_2024_1_P083 - Item 1 - Government Regulations > Environmental Compliance

[mu_2024_1_P084_S001] Parent mu_2024_1_P084 - Item 1 - Government Regulations > Trade Regulations

Any such laws or regulations may require that we either obtain licenses or other authorizations to export certain of our products or sell them to certain countries, companies, or individuals, or, in the absence of such licenses or authorizations, not export or sell the applicable products or transfer the related technical information and know-how to the affected countries, companies, or individuals.

[mu_2024_1_P084_S004] Parent mu_2024_1_P084 - Item 1 - Government Regulations > Trade Regulations

[mu_2024_1A_P011_S004] Parent mu_2024_1A_P011 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Our Business, Operations, and Industry

[mu_2024_1A_P026_S001] Parent mu_2024_1A_P026 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Our Business, Operations, and Industry

[mu_2024_1A_P027_S001] Parent mu_2024_1A_P027 - Item 1A - Risks Related to Our Business, Operations, and Industry

[mu_2024_7_P001_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P001 - Item 7 - Management's Discussion and Analysis
This discussion should be read in conjunction with the consolidated financial statements and accompanying notes for the year ended August 29, 2024.

[mu_2024_7_P001_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P001 - Item 7 - Management's Discussion and Analysis
All period references are to our fiscal periods unless otherwise indicated.

[mu_2024_7_P001_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P001 - Item 7 - Management's Discussion and Analysis
Our fiscal year is the 52 or 53-week period ending on the Thursday closest to August 31.

[mu_2024_7_P001_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P001 - Item 7 - Management's Discussion and Analysis
Fiscal 2024, 2023, and 2022 each contained 52 weeks.

[mu_2024_7_P001_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P001 - Item 7 - Management's Discussion and Analysis
All tabular dollar amounts are in millions, except per share amounts.

[mu_2024_7_P002_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P002 - Item 7 - Overview
For an overview of our business, see "Part I - Item 1. Business - Overview."

[mu_2024_7_P003_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P003 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Throughout 2024, we experienced substantial improvements in pricing and margins.

[mu_2024_7_P003_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P003 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Increasing demand growth, driven in part by deployment of AI and mostly normal customer inventories, combined with industry-wide supply discipline, resulted in an industry supply and demand balance that substantially improved from 2023 conditions.

[mu_2024_7_P003_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P003 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We executed well on pricing and improved our financial performance significantly from the start of the year.

[mu_2024_7_P003_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P003 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We are exiting the year with excellent momentum and an industry-leading product portfolio.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
In contrast, 2023 was a year of weak memory and storage industry demand in many end markets, stemming from global macroeconomic challenges and customer actions to reduce inventory levels.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
These conditions, which began in the fourth quarter of 2022 and persisted into early 2024, led to significant reductions in average selling prices for both DRAM and NAND and reductions in bit shipments for DRAM.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We experienced declines in revenue across all our business segments and nearly all our end markets throughout 2023.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Also in 2023, China's Cyberspace Administration (the "CAC") conducted a cybersecurity review of our products sold in China and decided that our products presented a cybersecurity risk.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
The CAC determined that critical information infrastructure operators in China may not purchase Micron products.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
The CAC decision has impacted our business, particularly in the domestic data center and networking markets in China, and we have been working to mitigate that impact.

[mu_2024_7_P004_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P004 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Our goal is to retain our worldwide DRAM and NAND market share.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
In manufacturing, we have been fully utilized throughout most of 2024 on our high-volume manufacturing nodes where we are maximizing output against our current capacity, which we have proactively, structurally lowered.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Beginning in the latter part of 2022, we reduced capital expenditures and wafer starts for both DRAM and NAND in response to challenging market conditions and increased levels of our inventories.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
In addition, to improve capital efficiency, we redeployed equipment from older technology nodes to support conversions to leading-edge nodes.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Since the number of wafer processing steps is higher for leading-edge nodes, this approach has resulted in a meaningful structural reduction in DRAM and NAND wafer capacity.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We believe this approach to node migration and consequent wafer capacity reduction was adopted across the industry.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We recognized period costs from fabrication facility underutilization of $382 million in 2023 and $165 million in the first quarter of 2024 due to wafer start reductions.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Subsequently, fabrication facility underutilization was reduced and principally related to legacy manufacturing capacity.

[mu_2024_7_P005_S008] Parent mu_2024_7_P005 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Accordingly, 2024 period costs beyond the first quarter were not significant.

[mu_2024_7_P006_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
In connection with improved 2024 market conditions, we reinstated our bonuses and phased out certain other temporary cost-saving measures that were implemented in 2023.

[mu_2024_7_P006_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We took significant steps in 2023 to reduce our costs and operating expenses, both on a temporary and ongoing structural basis.

[mu_2024_7_P006_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
These measures included the 2023 Restructure Plan, as well as implementing productivity programs, suspension of our 2023 bonus, reductions in select product programs, lower discretionary spending, and cuts to 2023 executive salaries.

[mu_2024_7_P006_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
Under the 2023 Restructure Plan, we reduced our headcount by approximately 15% by the end of calendar 2023, through a combination of voluntary attrition and personnel reductions.

[mu_2024_7_P006_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
We incurred restructure charges of $171 million in 2023 primarily related to employee severance costs.

[mu_2024_7_P006_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P006 - Item 7 - Overview > Industry Conditions
The 2023 Restructure Plan, which was substantially completed in 2023, yielded estimated cost savings of approximately $130 million per quarter (approximately 60% in cost of goods sold, 30% in R&D, and 10% in SG&A) subsequent to 2023.

[mu_2024_7_P007_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Total Revenue: Total revenue for 2024 and 2023 was impacted by the factors described in the section titled "Industry Conditions" above.

[mu_2024_7_P007_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P007 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
These conditions drove a recovery of average selling prices throughout 2024 after significant declines in average selling prices throughout 2023.

[mu_2024_7_P008_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P008 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Total revenue for 2024 increased 62% as compared to 2023 primarily due to increases in sales of both DRAM and NAND products.

[mu_2024_7_P008_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P008 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
• Sales of DRAM products increased 60% primarily due to a mid-40% range increase in bit shipments and a low-teen percentage range increase in average selling prices.

[mu_2024_7_P008_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P008 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
• Sales of NAND products increased 72% primarily due to a low-30% range increase in bit shipments and a low-30% percentage range increase in average selling prices.

[mu_2024_7_P009_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P009 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Total revenue for 2023 decreased 49% as compared to 2022 primarily due to decreases in sales of both DRAM and NAND products.

[mu_2024_7_P009_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P009 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
• Sales of DRAM products decreased 51% primarily due to a high-40% range decline in average selling prices and decreases in bit shipments in the high-single-digit percent range.

[mu_2024_7_P009_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P009 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
• Sales of NAND products decreased 46% primarily due to a low-50% range decline in average selling prices partially offset by increases in bit shipments in the high-single-digit percent range.

[mu_2024_7_P010_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P010 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Consolidated Gross Margin:Our consolidated gross margin has been impacted by the factors described in the section titled "Industry Conditions" above and inventory write-downs in 2023 as detailed in the table below.

[mu_2024_7_P010_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P010 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Our consolidated gross margin percentage improved to 22% for 2024 from negative 9% for 2023, as a result of improvements in margins for both DRAM and NAND products, primarily due to increases in average selling prices, and manufacturing cost reductions, the effects of charges to write down inventories to their NRV in 2023 and lower costs in 2024 from the sale of inventories written down in 2023 (as detailed in "Inventory NRV write-downs" below).

[mu_2024_7_P011_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P011 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Our consolidated gross margin percentage decreased to negative 9% for 2023 from 45% for 2022 primarily due to declines in average selling prices for both DRAM and NAND, charges to write down inventories, and $382 million of facility underutilization costs in 2023.

[mu_2024_7_P012_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
Inventory NRV write-downs:Our consolidated gross margin was impacted by charges in 2023 to write down inventories to their estimated NRV as a result of declines in average selling prices for both DRAM and NAND.

[mu_2024_7_P012_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
As charges to write down inventories are recorded in advance of when inventories are sold, costs of goods sold in subsequent periods were lower than they otherwise would be.

[mu_2024_7_P012_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P012 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Consolidated Results
The impact of inventory NRV write-downs for each period reflects (1) inventory write-downs in that period, offset by (2) lower costs in that period on the sale of inventory written down in prior periods.

[mu_2024_7_P019_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P019 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
Research and Development: R&D expenses vary primarily with the number of development and pre-qualification wafers processed, the cost of advanced equipment dedicated to new product and process development, and personnel costs.

[mu_2024_7_P019_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P019 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
Because of the lead times necessary to manufacture our products, we typically begin to process wafers before completion of performance and reliability testing.

[mu_2024_7_P019_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P019 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
Development of a product is deemed complete when it is qualified through internal reviews and tests for performance and reliability.

[mu_2024_7_P019_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P019 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
R&D expenses can vary significantly depending on the timing of product qualification.

[mu_2024_7_P020_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P020 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
R&D expenses for 2024 increased 10% as compared to 2023 primarily due to an increase in employee compensation and higher volumes of development and prequalification wafers, partially offset by an increase in government incentives.

[mu_2024_7_P020_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P020 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
R&D expenses for 2023 were relatively unchanged as compared to 2022 as decreases in employee compensation were offset by higher depreciation expense.

[mu_2024_7_P021_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P021 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
Selling, General, and Administrative: SG&A expenses for 2024 increased 23% as compared to 2023 primarily due to an increase in employee compensation.

[mu_2024_7_P021_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P021 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
SG&A expenses for 2023 were 14% lower as compared to 2022 primarily due to decreases in employee compensation, legal fees, advertising, and professional services.

[mu_2024_7_P022_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P022 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
Interest Income (Expense), Net: Interest income (expense) deteriorated for 2024 as compared to 2023 primarily due to increases in interest expense as a result of higher interest rates on our debt, partially offset by increases in interest income due to higher interest rates on our cash and investments.

[mu_2024_7_P022_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P022 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other
Interest income (expense) improved for 2023 as compared to 2022 primarily as a result of increases in interest income due to higher interest rates on our cash and investments, partially offset by increases in interest expense due to higher debt balances and interest rates on our debt.

[mu_2024_7_P025_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P025 - Item 7 - Results of Operations > Operating Expenses and Other

[mu_2024_7_P031_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
To support projected memory demand in the second half of the decade, we will need to add new DRAM wafer capacity.

[mu_2024_7_P031_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
Following the enactment of the U.S. CHIPS and Science Act of 2022 ("CHIPS Act"), we announced plans to invest in two leading-edge memory manufacturing fab facilities in the United States, based on CHIPS Act support through grants and investment tax credits.

[mu_2024_7_P031_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
As part of this plan, in September 2022, we broke ground on a leading-edge memory manufacturing fab in Boise, Idaho.

[mu_2024_7_P031_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
Construction of the fab began in October 2023, with meaningful DRAM output projected in 2027.

[mu_2024_7_P031_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
In addition, in October 2022, we announced plans to build a second leading-edge DRAM manufacturing facility, consisting of up to four fabs to be built over the next 20-plus years, in Clay, New York.

[mu_2024_7_P031_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
We expect construction site preparation to begin in calendar 2025, with production anticipated to ramp in the latter half of the decade.

[mu_2024_7_P031_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P031 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
We expect these new fabs to be key to meeting our requirements for additional wafer capacity starting in the second half of the decade and beyond, in line with industry demand trends and to maintain an objective of stable bit share.

[mu_2024_7_P032_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
We have signed a non-binding preliminary memorandum of terms with the U.S. Department of Commerce for up to $6.1 billion in direct funding under the CHIPS Act for our planned fab in Boise, Idaho and the first two planned fabs in Clay, New York.

[mu_2024_7_P032_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
We are also eligible for federal loans up to $7.5 billion.

[mu_2024_7_P032_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources

[mu_2024_7_P032_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P032 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
We have also signed a non-binding term sheet with the state of New York that provides up to $5.5 billion in funding for the planned four-fab facility over the next 20-plus years through a combination of tax credits for qualified capital investments and incentives for eligible new job wages.

[mu_2024_7_P033_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P033 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources
Additionally, we began enablement of cleanroom space within our existing manufacturing fab in Hiroshima, Japan, that will support production of DRAM using EUV lithography.

[mu_2024_7_P033_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P033 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources

[mu_2024_7_P033_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P033 - Item 7 - Liquidity and Capital Resources

The preparation of financial statements and related disclosures in conformity with U.S. GAAP requires management to make estimates and judgments that affect the reported amounts of assets, liabilities, revenues, expenses, and related disclosures.

[mu_2024_7_P047_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P047 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Estimates and judgments are based on historical experience, forecasted events, and various other assumptions that we believe to be reasonable under the circumstances.

[mu_2024_7_P047_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P047 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Estimates and judgments may vary under different assumptions or conditions and involve a significant level of uncertainty.

[mu_2024_7_P047_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P047 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We evaluate our estimates and judgments on an ongoing basis.

[mu_2024_7_P047_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P047 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Our management believes the accounting policies below are critical in the portrayal of our financial condition and results of operations and require management's most difficult, subjective, or complex judgments.

[mu_2024_7_P048_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P048 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Contingencies: We are subject to the possibility of losses from various contingencies.

[mu_2024_7_P048_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P048 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Significant judgment is necessary to estimate the probability and amount of a loss, if any, from such contingencies.

[mu_2024_7_P048_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P048 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
An accrual is made when it is probable that a liability has been incurred or an asset has been impaired, and the amount of loss can be reasonably estimated.

[mu_2024_7_P048_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P048 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
In accounting for the resolution of contingencies, significant judgment may be necessary to estimate amounts pertaining to periods prior to the resolution that are charged to operations in the period of resolution and amounts related to future periods.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Goodwill: We test goodwill for impairment in our fourth quarter each year, or more frequently if indicators of an impairment exist, to determine whether it is more likely than not that the fair value of the reporting unit with goodwill is less than its carrying value.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
For reporting units for which this assessment concludes that it is more likely than not that the fair value is more than its carrying value, goodwill is considered not impaired, and we are not required to perform the quantitative goodwill impairment test.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Qualitative factors considered in this assessment include industry and market considerations, overall financial performance, and other relevant events and factors affecting the fair value of the reporting unit.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
For reporting units for which this assessment concludes that it is more likely than not that the fair value is below the carrying value, goodwill is tested for impairment by determining the fair value of each reporting unit and comparing it to the carrying value of the net assets assigned to the reporting unit.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
If the fair value of the reporting unit exceeds its carrying value, goodwill is considered not impaired.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
If the carrying value of the reporting unit exceeds its fair value, we recognize an impairment loss up to the difference between the carrying value and implied fair value.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We recognized a charge of $101 million in 2023 to impair all of the goodwill assigned to our SBU reporting unit based on our quantitative assessment for impairment.

[mu_2024_7_P049_S008] Parent mu_2024_7_P049 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We performed a qualitative assessment for the current year and have not identified any impairment indicators for our reporting units.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Determining when to test for impairment, the reporting units, the assets and liabilities of the reporting unit, and the fair value of the reporting unit requires significant judgment and involves the use of significant estimates and assumptions.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
These estimates and assumptions include revenue growth rates, forecasted manufacturing costs, and other expenses and are developed as part of our long-range planning process.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
The same estimates are used in business planning, forecasting, and capital budgeting as part of our long-term manufacturing capacity analysis.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
These estimates and assumptions are used to calculate projected future cash flows for the reporting unit, which are discounted using a risk-adjusted rate to estimate a fair value.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
The discount rate requires determination of appropriate market comparables.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We base fair value estimates on assumptions we believe to be reasonable but that are unpredictable and inherently uncertain.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Actual future results may differ from those estimates.

[mu_2024_7_P050_S008] Parent mu_2024_7_P050 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We assess the reasonableness of our methodology, forecasts, and assumptions by comparing the aggregate calculated fair value for our reporting units to our market capitalization.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Income taxes: We are required to estimate our provision for income taxes and amounts ultimately payable or recoverable in numerous tax jurisdictions around the world.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
These estimates involve significant judgment and interpretations of regulations and are inherently complex.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Resolution of income tax treatments in individual jurisdictions may not be known for many years after completion of the applicable year.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We are also required to evaluate the realizability of our deferred tax assets on an ongoing basis in accordance with U.S. GAAP, which requires the assessment of our performance and other relevant factors.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Realization of deferred tax assets is dependent on our ability to generate future taxable income.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Our income tax provision or benefit is dependent, in part, on our ability to forecast future taxable income in Japan, the United States, Malaysia, Taiwan, and other jurisdictions.

[mu_2024_7_P051_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P051 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Such forecasts are inherently difficult and involve significant judgments including, among others, projecting future average selling prices and sales volumes, manufacturing and overhead costs, levels of capital spending, and other factors that significantly impact our analyses of the amount of net deferred tax assets that are more likely than not to be realized.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Inventories: Inventories are stated at the lower of cost or net realizable value, with cost being determined on a first-in, first-out ("FIFO") basis.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Cost includes depreciation, labor, material, and overhead costs, including product and process technology costs.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Determining net realizable value of finished goods and work in process inventories involves significant judgments, including projecting future average selling prices, future sales volumes, and future cost per part.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
To project average selling prices and sales volumes, we review recent sales volumes, existing customer orders, current contract prices, industry analyses of supply and demand, and general economic trends.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
To project cost per part, we review trends with historical results and consider known changes in our cost structure as applicable.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Actual selling prices may vary significantly from projected prices due to the volatile nature of the semiconductor memory and storage markets.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S007] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
When these analyses reflect estimated net realizable values below our manufacturing costs, we record a charge to cost of goods sold in advance of when inventories are actually sold.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S008] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
As a result, the timing of when product costs are charged to costs of goods sold can vary significantly.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S009] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Differences in future average selling prices used in calculating lower of cost or net realizable value adjustments can result in significant changes in the estimated net realizable value of finished goods and work in process inventories and accordingly the amount of write-down recorded.

[mu_2024_7_P052_S010] Parent mu_2024_7_P052 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
For example, a 5% decrease in future average selling prices would have changed the estimated net realizable value of our finished goods and work in process inventories by approximately $700 million as of August 29, 2024.

[mu_2024_7_P053_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P053 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
U.S. GAAP provides for products to be grouped into categories in order to compare costs to net realizable values.

[mu_2024_7_P053_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P053 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
The amount of any inventory write-down can vary significantly depending on the determination of inventory categories.

[mu_2024_7_P053_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P053 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We review the major characteristics of product type and markets in determining the unit of account for which we perform the lower of cost or net realizable value analysis and categorize all inventories (including DRAM, NAND, and other memory) as a single group.

[mu_2024_7_P054_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P054 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Property, plant, and equipment: We periodically assess the estimated useful lives of our property, plant, and equipment based on technology node transitions, capital spending, and equipment re-use rates.

[mu_2024_7_P054_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P054 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We also review the carrying value of property, plant, and equipment for impairment when events and circumstances indicate that the carrying value of an asset or group of assets may not be recoverable from the estimated future cash flows expected to result from its use and/or disposition.

[mu_2024_7_P054_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P054 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
In cases where undiscounted expected future cash flows are less than the carrying value, an impairment loss is recognized equal to the amount by which the carrying value exceeds the estimated fair value of the assets.

[mu_2024_7_P054_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P054 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
The estimate of future cash flows involves numerous assumptions which require significant judgment by us, including, but not limited to, future use of the assets for our operations versus sale or disposal of the assets, future selling prices for our products, and future production and sales volumes.

[mu_2024_7_P055_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P055 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Revenue recognition: Revenue is primarily recognized at a point in time when control of the promised goods is transferred to our customers in an amount that reflects the consideration we expect to be entitled to in exchange for those goods.

[mu_2024_7_P055_S002] Parent mu_2024_7_P055 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Contracts with our customers are generally short-term in duration at fixed, negotiated prices with payment generally due shortly after delivery.

[mu_2024_7_P055_S003] Parent mu_2024_7_P055 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We estimate a liability for returns using the expected value method based on historical returns.

[mu_2024_7_P055_S004] Parent mu_2024_7_P055 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
In addition, we generally offer price protection to our distributors, which is a form of variable consideration that decreases the transaction price.

[mu_2024_7_P055_S005] Parent mu_2024_7_P055 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
We use the expected value method, based on historical price adjustments and current pricing trends, to estimate the amount of revenue recognized from sales to distributors.

[mu_2024_7_P055_S006] Parent mu_2024_7_P055 - Item 7 - Critical Accounting Estimates
Differences between the estimated and actual amounts are recognized as adjustments to revenue.

[mu_2024_7_P056_S001] Parent mu_2024_7_P056 - Item 7 - Recently Issued Accounting Standards
See "Item 8. Financial Statements and Supplementary Data - Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements - Recently Issued Accounting Standards."

[mu_2024_8_P013_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P013 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
We are an industry leader in innovative memory and storage solutions transforming how the world uses information to enrich life for all.

[mu_2024_8_P013_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P013 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
With a relentless focus on our customers, technology leadership, and manufacturing and operational excellence, Micron delivers a rich portfolio of high-performance DRAM, NAND, and NOR memory and storage products through our Micron® and Crucial® brands.

[mu_2024_8_P013_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P013 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
Every day, the innovations that our people create fuel the data economy, enabling advances in artificial intelligence (AI) and compute-intensive applications that unleash opportunities - from the data center to the intelligent edge and across the client and mobile user experience.

[mu_2024_8_P014_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P014 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
The accompanying consolidated financial statements include the accounts of Micron Technology, Inc. and our consolidated subsidiaries and have been prepared in accordance with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America.Intercompany balances and transactions have been eliminated in consolidation.Certain reclassifications have been made to prior period amounts to conform to current period presentation.

[mu_2024_8_P015_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P015 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
Our fiscal year is the 52 or 53-week period ending on the Thursday closest to August 31.

[mu_2024_8_P015_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P015 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
Fiscal 2024, 2023, and 2022 each contained 52 weeks.

[mu_2024_8_P015_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P015 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Basis of Presentation
All period references are to our fiscal periods unless otherwise indicated.

[mu_2024_8_P016_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P016 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
We use derivative instruments to manage our exposure to changes in currency exchange rates from (1) our monetary assets and liabilities denominated in currencies other than the U.S. dollar, (2) non-U.S.-dollar-denominated investments in debt instruments, and (3) forecasted cash flows for certain capital expenditures and manufacturing costs.

[mu_2024_8_P016_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P016 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
We also use derivative instruments to manage our exposure to changes in commodity prices for manufacturing supplies and to minimize certain exposures to changes in the fair value of fixed-rate debt that result from fluctuations in benchmark interest rates.

[mu_2024_8_P016_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P016 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
Derivative instruments are measured at their fair values and recognized as either assets or liabilities.

[mu_2024_8_P017_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P017 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
The accounting for changes in the fair value of derivative instruments is based on the intended use of the derivative and the resulting designation.

[mu_2024_8_P017_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P017 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
For derivative instruments that are not designated for hedge accounting, gains or losses from changes in fair values are recognized in other non-operating income (expense) and cash flows are classified as investing activities in the statement of cash flows.

[mu_2024_8_P017_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P017 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
For derivative instruments designated as cash flow hedges, gains or losses are included as a component of accumulated other comprehensive income and reclassified into earnings in the same line items and in the same periods in which the underlying transactions affect earnings.

[mu_2024_8_P017_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P017 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
For derivative instruments designated as fair value hedges, changes in the fair values of the derivative instruments and the offsetting changes in the fair values of the underlying hedged items are both recognized in earnings.

[mu_2024_8_P017_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P017 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
For foreign currency and commodity derivative instruments designated as cash flow hedges or fair value hedges, time value is excluded from the assessment of effectiveness and the gains and losses attributable to time value are recognized in earnings through an amortization approach.

[mu_2024_8_P017_S006] Parent mu_2024_8_P017 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
Cash flows from derivative instruments designated as cash flow hedges or fair value hedges are classified in the same category as the items being hedged.

[mu_2024_8_P018_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P018 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
We enter into master netting arrangements with our counterparties to mitigate credit risk in derivative hedge transactions.

[mu_2024_8_P018_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P018 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
These master netting arrangements allow us and our counterparties to net settle amounts owed to each other.

[mu_2024_8_P018_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P018 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Derivative and Hedging Instruments
Derivative assets and liabilities that can be net settled with each counterparty have been presented in our consolidated balance sheet on a net basis.

[mu_2024_8_P019_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P019 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Financial Instruments
Cash equivalents include highly liquid short-term investments with original maturities at the time of purchase of three months or less that are readily convertible to known amounts of cash.

[mu_2024_8_P019_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P019 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Financial Instruments
Other investments with remaining maturities of less than one year are included in short-term investments.

[mu_2024_8_P019_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P019 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Financial Instruments
Investments with remaining maturities greater than one year are included in long-term marketable investments.

[mu_2024_8_P019_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P019 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Financial Instruments
The carrying value of investment securities sold is determined using the specific identification method.

[mu_2024_8_P020_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P020 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Functional Currency
The U.S. dollar is the functional currency for us and all of our consolidated subsidiaries.

[mu_2024_8_P021_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P021 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Goodwill
We perform an annual impairment assessment for goodwill in our fourth quarter each year.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
We receive incentives from governmental entities related to capital expenditures, expenses, and other activities.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
Our government incentives may require that we meet or maintain specified spending levels and other operational metrics and may be subject to reimbursement if such conditions are not met or maintained.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
Government incentives are recorded in the financial statements in accordance with their purpose: as a reduction of asset costs or a reduction of expenses.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
Incentives related to the acquisition or construction of fixed assets are recognized as a reduction in the carrying amounts of the related assets and as a reduction to depreciation expense over the useful lives of the assets.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
Incentives related to specific operating activities are offset against the related expense in the period the expense is incurred.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S006] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
Government incentives received prior to being earned are recognized in current or noncurrent deferred income, whereas government incentives earned prior to being received are recognized in current or noncurrent receivables.

[mu_2024_8_P022_S007] Parent mu_2024_8_P022 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Government Incentives
Cash received from government incentives related to operating expenses is included as an operating activity in the statement of cash flows, whereas cash received from incentives related to the acquisition of property, plant, and equipment is included as an investing activity.

[mu_2024_8_P023_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P023 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Inventories
Inventories are stated at the lower of cost or net realizable value, with cost being determined on a FIFO basis.

[mu_2024_8_P023_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P023 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Inventories
Cost includes depreciation, labor, material, and overhead costs, including product and process technology costs.

[mu_2024_8_P023_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P023 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Inventories
Determining net realizable value of finished goods and work in process inventories requires projecting future average selling prices, sales volumes, and costs per part.

[mu_2024_8_P023_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P023 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Inventories
When net realizable value is below cost, we record a charge to cost of goods sold to write down inventories to their estimated net realizable value in advance of when inventories are actually sold.

[mu_2024_8_P023_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P023 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Inventories
We review the major characteristics of product type and markets in determining the unit of account for which we perform the lower of cost or net realizable value analysis and categorize all inventories (including DRAM, NAND, and other memory) as a single group.

[mu_2024_8_P024_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P024 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Leases
We determine if an arrangement is a lease, or contains a lease, at the inception of the arrangement and evaluate whether the lease is an operating lease or a finance lease at the commencement date.

[mu_2024_8_P024_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P024 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Leases
We recognize right-of-use assets and lease liabilities for operating and finance leases with terms greater than 12 months.

[mu_2024_8_P024_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P024 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Leases
Right-of-use assets represent our right to use an asset for the lease term, while lease liabilities represent our obligation to make lease payments.

[mu_2024_8_P024_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P024 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Leases
We do not separate lease and non-lease components for real-estate and gas plant leases.

[mu_2024_8_P024_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P024 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Leases
Sublease income is included within lease expense.

[mu_2024_8_P025_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P025 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product and Process Technology
Costs incurred to (1) acquire product and process technology, (2) patent technology, and (3) keep patents in force, are capitalized and amortized on a straight-line basis over periods ranging up to 12.5 years.

[mu_2024_8_P025_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P025 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product and Process Technology
We capitalize a portion of costs incurred to patent technology based on historical data of patents issued as a percent of patents we file.

[mu_2024_8_P025_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P025 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product and Process Technology
Product and process technology costs are amortized over the shorter of (1) the estimated useful life of the technology, (2) the patent term, or (3) the term of the technology agreement.

[mu_2024_8_P025_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P025 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product and Process Technology
Fully-amortized assets are removed from product and process technology and accumulated amortization.

[mu_2024_8_P026_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P026 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product Warranty
We generally provide a limited warranty that our products are in compliance with applicable specifications existing at the time of delivery.

[mu_2024_8_P026_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P026 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product Warranty
Under our standard terms and conditions of sale, liability for certain failures of product during a stated warranty period is usually limited to repair or replacement of defective items or return of, or a credit with respect to, amounts paid for such items.

[mu_2024_8_P026_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P026 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product Warranty
Under certain circumstances, we provide more extensive limited warranty coverage than that provided under our standard terms and conditions.

[mu_2024_8_P026_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P026 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Product Warranty
Our warranty obligations are not material.

[mu_2024_8_P027_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P027 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Property, Plant, and Equipment
Property, plant, and equipment is stated at cost and depreciated using the straight-line method over estimated useful lives of generally 10 to 30 years for buildings, 7 years for production equipment, up to 7 years for other equipment, and 3 to 5 years for software.

[mu_2024_8_P027_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P027 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Property, Plant, and Equipment
Assets held for sale are carried at the lower of estimated fair value or carrying value and are included in current assets.

[mu_2024_8_P027_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P027 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Property, Plant, and Equipment
When property, plant, or equipment is retired or otherwise disposed, the net book value is removed and we recognize any gain or loss in results of operations.

[mu_2024_8_P028_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P028 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Property, Plant, and Equipment
We capitalize interest on borrowings during the period of time we carry out the activities necessary to bring assets to the condition of their intended use and location.

[mu_2024_8_P028_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P028 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Property, Plant, and Equipment
We utilize a weighted average capitalization rate that is based on our consolidated debt portfolio.

[mu_2024_8_P028_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P028 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Property, Plant, and Equipment
Capitalized interest becomes part of the cost of assets.

[mu_2024_8_P029_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P029 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Research and Development
Costs related to the conceptual formulation and design of products and processes are charged to R&D expense as incurred.

[mu_2024_8_P029_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P029 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Research and Development
Development of a product is deemed complete when it is qualified through reviews and tests for performance and reliability.

[mu_2024_8_P029_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P029 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Research and Development
Subsequent to product qualification, product costs are included in cost of goods sold.

[mu_2024_8_P030_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P030 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Revenue Recognition
Revenue is primarily recognized at a point in time when control of the promised goods is transferred to our customers in an amount that reflects the consideration we expect to be entitled to in exchange for those goods.

[mu_2024_8_P030_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P030 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Revenue Recognition
Contracts with our customers are generally short-term in duration at fixed, negotiated prices with payment generally due shortly after delivery.

[mu_2024_8_P030_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P030 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Revenue Recognition
We estimate a liability for returns using the expected value method based on historical returns.

[mu_2024_8_P030_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P030 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Revenue Recognition
In addition, we generally offer price protection to our distributors, which is a form of variable consideration that decreases the transaction price.

[mu_2024_8_P030_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P030 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Revenue Recognition
We use the expected value method, based on historical price adjustments and current pricing trends, to estimate the amount of revenue recognized from sales to distributors.

[mu_2024_8_P030_S006] Parent mu_2024_8_P030 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Revenue Recognition
Differences between the estimated and actual amounts are recognized as adjustments to revenue.

[mu_2024_8_P031_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P031 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Stock-based Compensation
Stock-based compensation is measured at the grant date, based on the fair value of the award, and recognized as expense under the straight-line attribution method over the requisite service period.

[mu_2024_8_P031_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P031 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Stock-based Compensation
We account for forfeitures as they occur.

[mu_2024_8_P031_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P031 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Stock-based Compensation
We issue new shares upon the exercise of stock options, conversion of share units, or issuance of shares under our ESPP.

[mu_2024_8_P032_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P032 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Treasury Stock
Treasury stock is carried at cost.

[mu_2024_8_P032_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P032 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Treasury Stock
When we retire our treasury stock, any excess of the repurchase price paid over par value is allocated between additional capital and retained earnings.

[mu_2024_8_P033_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P033 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Use of Estimates
The preparation of financial statements and related disclosures in conformity with accounting principles generally accepted in the United States of America requires our management to make estimates and judgments that affect the reported amounts of assets, liabilities, revenues, expenses, and related disclosures.

[mu_2024_8_P033_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P033 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Use of Estimates
Estimates and judgments are based on historical experience, forecasted events, and various other assumptions that we believe to be reasonable under the circumstances.

[mu_2024_8_P033_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P033 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Use of Estimates
Estimates and judgments may differ under different assumptions or conditions.

[mu_2024_8_P033_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P033 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Use of Estimates
We evaluate our estimates and judgments on an ongoing basis.

[mu_2024_8_P033_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P033 - Item 8 - Significant Accounting Policies > Use of Estimates
Actual results could differ from estimates.

[mu_2024_8_P036_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P036 - Item 8 - Variable Interest Entities
A number of special purpose entities (the "Lease SPEs") were created by a third-party to facilitate equipment lease financing transactions between us and financial institutions that fund the lease financing transactions ("Financing Entities").

[mu_2024_8_P036_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P036 - Item 8 - Variable Interest Entities
Neither we nor the Financing Entities have an equity interest in the Lease SPEs.

[mu_2024_8_P036_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P036 - Item 8 - Variable Interest Entities
The Lease SPEs are variable interest entities because their equity is not sufficient to permit them to finance their activities without additional support from the Financing Entities and because the third-party equity holder lacks characteristics of a controlling financial interest.

[mu_2024_8_P036_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P036 - Item 8 - Variable Interest Entities
By design, the arrangements with the Lease SPEs are merely financing vehicles and we do not bear any significant risks from variable interests with the Lease SPEs.

[mu_2024_8_P036_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P036 - Item 8 - Variable Interest Entities
We have determined that we do not have the power to direct the activities of the Lease SPEs that most significantly impact their economic performance and we do not consolidate the Lease SPEs.

[mu_2024_8_P036_S006] Parent mu_2024_8_P036 - Item 8 - Variable Interest Entities
As of August 29, 2024, we had approximately $680 million of financial lease liabilities and right-of-use assets under these arrangements.

[mu_2024_8_P047_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P047 - Item 8 - Leases

[mu_2024_8_P047_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P047 - Item 8 - Leases

[mu_2024_8_P047_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P047 - Item 8 - Leases

[mu_2024_8_P048_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P048 - Item 8 - Leases
Certain supply or service agreements require us to exercise significant judgment to determine whether the agreement contains a lease.

[mu_2024_8_P048_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P048 - Item 8 - Leases
Our assessment includes determining whether we or the supplier control the assets used to fulfill the agreements by identifying whether we or the supplier have the right to change the type, quantity, timing, or location of the output of the assets.

[mu_2024_8_P048_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P048 - Item 8 - Leases
Our gas supply arrangements generally are deemed to contain a lease because we have the right to substantially all of the output of the assets used to produce the supply and we have the right to change the quantity and timing of the output of those assets.

[mu_2024_8_P048_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P048 - Item 8 - Leases
In determining the lease term, we assess whether we are reasonably certain to exercise any options to renew or terminate a lease or to purchase the right-of-use asset.

[mu_2024_8_P048_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P048 - Item 8 - Leases
Measuring the present value of the initial lease liability requires judgment to determine the discount rate, which we base on interest rates for borrowings with similar terms and collateral issued by entities with credit ratings similar to ours.

[mu_2024_8_P056_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P056 - Item 8 - Debt > Senior Unsecured Notes

[mu_2024_8_P057_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P057 - Item 8 - Debt > Senior Unsecured Notes
Each series of Senior Unsecured Notes contains covenants that, among other things, limit, in certain circumstances, our ability and/or the ability of our restricted subsidiaries (which are generally domestic subsidiaries in which we own at least 80% of the voting stock and which own principal property, as defined in the indenture governing such series) to (1) create or incur certain liens; (2) enter into certain sale and lease-back transactions; and (3) consolidate with or merge with or into, or convey, transfer, or lease all or substantially all of our properties and assets, to another entity.

[mu_2024_8_P057_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P057 - Item 8 - Debt > Senior Unsecured Notes

[mu_2024_8_P057_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P057 - Item 8 - Debt > Senior Unsecured Notes

[mu_2024_8_P058_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P058 - Item 8 - Debt > Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement

[mu_2024_8_P058_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P058 - Item 8 - Debt > Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement

[mu_2024_8_P059_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P059 - Item 8 - Debt > Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement
The Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement requires us to maintain, on a consolidated basis, a leverage ratio of total indebtedness to adjusted EBITDA, as defined in the Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement and calculated as of the last day of each fiscal quarter, not to exceed 3.25 to 1.00, subject to a temporary four quarter increase in such ratio to 3.75 to 1.00 following certain material acquisitions.

[mu_2024_8_P060_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P060 - Item 8 - Debt > Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement
The Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement contains other covenants that, among other things, limit, in certain circumstances, our ability and/or the ability of our restricted subsidiaries to (1) create or incur certain liens and enter into sale and lease-back transactions, (2) create, assume, incur, or guarantee certain additional secured indebtedness and unsecured indebtedness of our restricted subsidiaries, and (3) consolidate with or merge with or into, or convey, transfer, lease, or otherwise dispose of all or substantially all of our assets, to another entity.

[mu_2024_8_P060_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P060 - Item 8 - Debt > Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement
These covenants are subject to a number of limitations, exceptions, and qualifications.

[mu_2024_8_P060_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P060 - Item 8 - Debt > Multi-Tranche Term Loan Agreement

[mu_2024_8_P066_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P066 - Item 8 - Contingencies

[mu_2024_8_P067_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P067 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
As is typical in the semiconductor and other high-tech industries, from time to time, others have asserted, and may in the future assert, that our products or manufacturing processes infringe upon their intellectual property rights.

A description of certain claims is below.

[mu_2024_8_P068_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P068 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On April 28, 2021, Netlist, Inc. ("Netlist") filed two patent infringement actions against Micron, Micron Semiconductor Products, Inc. ("MSP"), and Micron Technology Texas, LLC ("MTEC") in the U.S. District Court for the Western District of Texas.

[mu_2024_8_P068_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P068 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
The first complaint alleges that one U.S. patent is infringed by certain of our non-volatile dual in-line memory modules.

[mu_2024_8_P068_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P068 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
The second complaint alleges that three U.S. patents are infringed by certain of our load-reduced dual in-line memory modules ("LRDIMMs").

[mu_2024_8_P068_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P068 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
Each complaint seeks injunctive relief, damages, attorneys' fees, and costs.

[mu_2024_8_P068_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P068 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On March 31, 2022, Netlist filed a patent infringement complaint against Micron and Micron Semiconductor Germany, GmbH in Dusseldorf Regional Court alleging that two German patents are infringed by certain of our LRDIMMs. The complaint seeks damages, costs, and injunctive relief.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S001] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On June 10, 2022, Netlist filed a patent infringement complaint against Micron, MSP, and MTEC in the U.S. District Court for the Eastern District of Texas ("E.D. Tex.") alleging that six U.S. patents are infringed by certain of our memory modules and HBM products.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On August 1, 2022, Netlist filed a second patent infringement complaint against the same defendants in E.D. Tex. alleging that one U.S. patent is infringed by certain of our LRDIMMs. On August 15, 2022, Netlist amended the second complaint to assert that two additional U.S. patents are infringed by certain of our LRDIMMs. The complaints in E.D. Tex. seek injunctive relief, damages, and attorneys' fees.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S003] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On May 23, 2024, following a four-day trial regarding the second complaint filed by Netlist in the E.D. Tex., a jury rendered a verdict that Micron's memory modules infringe two asserted patents - U.S. Patent No. 7,619,912 ("the '912 patent") and U.S. Patent No. 11,093,417 ("the '417 patent") - and found that Micron should pay $425 million for infringement of the '912 patent and $20 million for infringement of the '417 patent.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S004] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
Micron expects to appeal the verdict.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S005] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On April 17, 2024, the Patent Trial and Appeal Board ("PTAB") of the United States Patent and Trademark Office ("USPTO") issued a final written decision ("FWD") finding unpatentable the sole asserted claim of the '912 patent.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S006] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
Netlist sought review of that ruling by the Director of the USPTO, which was denied on July 10, 2024.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S007] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
On July 30, 2024, the USPTO issued a FWD finding unpatentable all asserted claims of the '417 patent.

[mu_2024_8_P069_S008] Parent mu_2024_8_P069 - Item 8 - Contingencies > Patent Matters
In the case of each of the '912 and '417 patents, if the United States Court of Appeals for the Federal Circuit affirms the FWD, then the affirmed FWD will preclude any pending actions asserting infringement of such patent (including any infringement verdict that is subject to an ongoing appeal).

Currency Derivatives: We generally utilize a rolling hedge strategy with currency forward contracts that mature within three months to hedge our exposures of monetary assets and liabilities from changes in currency exchange rates.

[mu_2024_8_P091_S002] Parent mu_2024_8_P091 - Item 8 - Derivative Instruments > Derivative Instruments without Hedge Accounting Designation
At the end of each reporting period, monetary assets and liabilities denominated in currencies other than the U.S. dollar are remeasured into U.S. dollars and the associated outstanding forward contracts are marked to market.
